=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
There is a grid of H \times W cells. Let (i, j) denote the cell at the i-th row from the top and the j-th column from the left.
Cell (i, j) is empty if S_{i,j} is ., and blocked if it is #.
Count the number of ways to start from an empty cell and make K moves to adjacent cells (up, down, left, or right), without passing through blocked squares and not visiting the same cell more than once.
Specifically, count the number of sequences of length K+1, ((i_0, j_0), (i_1, j_1), \dots, (i_K, j_K)), satisfying the following.

- 1 \leq i_k \leq H, 1 \leq j_k \leq W, and S_{i_k, j_k} is ., for each 0 \leq k \leq K.
- |i_{k+1} - i_k| + |j_{k+1} - j_k| = 1 for each 0 \leq k \leq K-1.
- (i_k, j_k) \neq (i_l, j_l) for each 0 \leq k < l \leq K.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
H W K
S_{1,1}S_{1,2}\dots S_{1,W}
S_{2,1}S_{2,2}\dots S_{2,W}
\vdots
S_{H,1}S_{H,2}\dots S_{H,W}

Output

Print the answer.

Constraints


- 1 \leq H, W \leq 10
- 1 \leq K \leq 11
- H, W, and K are integers.
- Each S_{i,j} is . or #.
- There is at least one empty cell.

Sample Input 1

2 2 2
.#
..

Sample Output 1

2

Here are the two possible paths:

- (1,1) \rightarrow (2,1) \rightarrow (2,2)
- (2,2) \rightarrow (2,1) \rightarrow (1,1)

Sample Input 2

2 3 1
.#.
#.#

Sample Output 2

0

Sample Input 3

10 10 11
....#..#..
.#.....##.
..#...##..
...#......
......##..
..#......#
#........#
..##......
.###....#.
...#.....#

Sample Output 3

218070

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Could optimize by precomputing neighbor list for each cell to avoid repeated boundary checks and char checks. Let's design final code:

import sys
def main():
    input=sys.stdin.readline
    H,W,K=map(int,input().split())
    S=[input().strip() for _ in range(H)]
    dirs=[(1,0),(-1,0),(0,1),(0,-1)]
    # precompute neighbors for each cell index
    neighbors = [[] for _ in range(H*W)]
    empty_cells=[]
    for i in range(H):
        for j in range(W):
            if S[i][j]=='.':
                idx=i*W+j
                empty_cells.append((i,j))
                for di,dj in dirs:
                    ni,nj=i+di,j+dj
                    if 0<=ni<H and 0<=nj<W and S[ni][nj]=='.':
                        neighbors[idx].append(ni*W+nj)
    N=H*W
    visited=[False]*N
    ans=0
    sys.setrecursionlimit(10000)
    def dfs(v, depth):
        nonlocal ans
        if depth == K:
            ans += 1
            return
        for to in neighbors[v]:
            if not visited[to]:
                visited[to]=True
                dfs(to, depth+1)
                visited[to]=False
    for i,j in empty_cells:
        v=i*W+j
        visited[v]=True
        dfs(v,0)
        visited[v]=False
    print(ans)
if __name__=="__main__": main()

This uses visited as list of bool length N, reused. Since after each start we set visited[v]=False. For each DFS, visited marks current path. At start, set true, after dfs false. Good.

MITM algorithm details:

1. Read H,W,K, grid.
2. Map each cell to index 0..N-1, bit = 1<<idx. N<=100, Python int bitmask okay.
3. Precompute neighbors for each empty cell (list of neighbor indices).
4. Function enumerate_walks(L):
   - Returns a list of dictionaries? We need for each start? Actually for prefix and suffix grouping by mid.
   - For each start cell v (empty):
       DFS from v, depth 0, visited mask = bit[v], path? We need record when depth == L: end = current cell, mask = visited_mask (or mask_without_start?).
       For prefix: group by end (mid), store mask_without_end? Wait condition uses prefix and suffix share only mid. If we store prefix mask including start..mid, and suffix mask including mid..end, condition prefix_mask & suffix_mask == mid_bit. We can store prefix_mask_without_mid = visited_mask ^ bit[mid] (since mid is current end). For suffix, store suffix_mask_without_mid = visited_mask ^ bit[start] (since start=mid). Then condition (maskP & maskQ)==0.
       For enumeration, we can record (end, mask_without_end) for prefix; (start, mask_without_start) for suffix. But if we use same enumerate function, for prefix we need group by end and mask without end; for suffix group by start and mask without start.
       We can implement two functions or parameterize.

   - enumerate_prefix(L):
       For each start v, DFS L steps. At depth L, current cell u. Add to pref_by_end[u][visited_mask ^ bit[u]] += 1. Because mid = u. The mask without mid includes start and intermediate cells.
       Note: visited_mask includes all cells in prefix. bit[u] is mid. Good.
   - enumerate_suffix(L):
       For each start v (mid), DFS L steps. At depth L, current cell u (end). Add to suff_by_start[v][visited_mask ^ bit[v]] += 1. Because mask without start includes end and intermediate cells.
       Note: For suffix, the path starts at mid v and ends at u. The mask without start includes all other cells. Good.

   We can implement a generic DFS that at depth L records:
       if mode == 'prefix': group_by[end][mask ^ bit[end]] += 1
       if mode == 'suffix': group_by[start][mask ^ bit[start]] += 1
   But start is the initial cell of the walk. For prefix, start is full start; for suffix, start is mid. We can pass group_by and mask_remove bit.

   Since L <= 6, recursion depth small. We can enumerate all walks length L from every empty cell.

   Pseudocode:
   def enumerate_walks(L, remove_start=False):
       groups = [dict() for _ in range(N)]  # group by endpoint if prefix, by start if suffix
       for s in empty_cells:
           def dfs(v, depth, mask):
               if depth == L:
                   if remove_start:
                       # suffix: group by s, mask without s
                       d = groups[s]
                       key = mask ^ (1<<s)
                       d[key] = d.get(key,0)+1
                   else:
                       # prefix: group by v, mask without v
                       d = groups[v]
                       key = mask ^ (1<<v)
                       d[key] = d.get(key,0)+1
                   return
               for to in neighbors[v]:
                   if not (mask >> to) & 1:
                       dfs(to, depth+1, mask | (1<<to))
           dfs(s, 0, 1<<s)
       return groups
   But for suffix, remove_start=True, group by start s. For prefix, remove_start=False, group by current v. This works. Note: For prefix, if L=0, at depth==0, v=s, key = mask ^ bit[v] = 0. groups[s][0] += 1. This represents a prefix of length 0 at mid=s. Good. For suffix L=0, remove_start=True, groups[s][0] +=1. Good.

   However, in DFS, for prefix, when depth==L, current v is mid. key = mask ^ bit[v] removes mid. For suffix, when depth==L, current v is end, start=s. key = mask ^ bit[s] removes start (mid). Good.

   But note: For suffix, the mask includes start s and all visited cells. We remove s. The key includes end and intermediate. Good.

   Need ensure that for prefix, the mask includes start and mid; removing mid leaves start and intermediates. For suffix, removing start leaves end and intermediates. In full path, prefix and suffix share only mid. If prefix key and suffix key are disjoint, then full path has no overlap except mid. Good.

5. Choose split:
   a = K // 2
   b = K - a
   For K=11, a=5,b=6. For K=10, a=5,b=5. For K=1, a=0,b=1. For K=2, a=1,b=1. This split ensures b>=a and b<=6? Since K<=11, b = ceil(K/2) <=6. a<=5. Good. So enumeration length <=6, very small.

6. Count:
   pref = enumerate_walks(a, remove_start=False)
   suff = enumerate_walks(b, remove_start=True)
   ans = 0
   for mid in range(N):
       if not empty? groups empty if no walks.
       dpref = pref[mid]  # prefix paths ending at mid: key mask without mid -> count
       dsuff = suff[mid]  # suffix paths starting at mid: key mask without mid -> count
       For each (maskP, cp) in dpref.items():
           For each (maskQ, cq) in dsuff.items():
               if maskP & maskQ == 0:
                   ans += cp * cq
   Return ans.

Implementation details for enumerate_walks:

def enumerate_walks(L, remove_start):
    groups = [dict() for _ in range(N)]
    # local variables for speed
    neigh = neighbors
    for s in empty_indices:
        start_bit = 1 << s
        def dfs(v, depth, mask):
            if depth == L:
                if remove_start:
                    key = mask ^ start_bit
                    d = groups[s]
                else:
                    key = mask ^ (1 << v)
                    d = groups[v]
                d[key] = d.get(key, 0) + 1
                return
            for to in neigh[v]:
                if not (mask >> to) & 1:
                    dfs(to, depth + 1, mask | (1 << to))
        dfs(s, 0, start_bit)
    return groups

But defining dfs inside loop over s captures s and start_bit; okay. However for each start, recursive function defined anew, overhead small. Could optimize by defining a nested function with parameters s, start_bit? But fine.

Potential issue: For remove_start=False (prefix), if L=0, at depth==0, key = mask ^ (1<<v) = 0, groups[v][0]+=1. Good. For remove_start=True (suffix), if L=0, key = mask ^ start_bit =0, groups[s][0]+=1. Good.

Potential issue: For suffix, when depth==L, current v is end. key = mask ^ start_bit. The mask includes start_bit and all visited. Removing start_bit leaves end and intermediates. Good.

Potential issue: For prefix, when depth==L, current v is mid. key = mask ^ (1<<v). The mask includes start and mid; removing mid leaves start and intermediates. Good.

    # Iterate smaller outer? For speed, if len(dpref) > len(dsuff), swap? Condition symmetric (maskP & maskQ ==0), so we can iterate over smaller outer to reduce overhead? Pair checks same, but Python loop overhead maybe. We can do:

        for mp, cp in dpref.items():

                    ans += cp * cq
    else:
        for mq, cq in dsuff.items():
            for mp, cp in dpref.items():
                if mp & mq == 0:
                    ans += cp * cq
But product same. Could optimize by if one dict empty skip.

Precompute neighbors:
neighbors = [[] for _ in range(N)]
empty_indices = []
for i in range(H):
    for j in range(W):
        if S[i][j] == '.':
            idx = i*W+j
            empty_indices.append(idx)
            for di,dj in dirs:
                ni,nj = i+di, j+dj
                if 0<=ni<H and 0<=nj<W and S[ni][nj]=='.':
                    neighbors[idx].append(ni*W+nj)

bit = [1 << i for i in range(N)]

enumerate_walks:
def enumerate_walks(L, remove_start):
    groups = [dict() for _ in range(N)]
    neigh = neighbors
    bits = bit
    for s in empty_indices:
        start_bit = bits[s]
        def dfs(v, depth, mask):
            if depth == L:
                if remove_start:
                    key = mask ^ start_bit
                    d = groups[s]
                else:
                    key = mask ^ bits[v]
                    d = groups[v]
                d[key] = d.get(key, 0) + 1
                return
            for to in neigh[v]:
                if not (mask & bits[to]):
                    dfs(to, depth + 1, mask | bits[to])
        dfs(s, 0, start_bit)
    return groups

Potential issue: The inner dfs uses `remove_start` from outer; if remove_start is True, group by s. For prefix, group by v. Good.

Could further optimize by not defining dfs for each s? We can define a recursive function with parameters s, start_bit, remove_start? But then each call passes extra args. Not necessary.

However, there is a subtle bug: In Python, closures capture variables by reference. The inner dfs uses `s` and `start_bit` from the for loop. Since dfs is called synchronously before s changes, no issue. The function object is discarded after loop iteration? Actually dfs is defined inside loop and called immediately; after call returns, no reference remains. So okay.

Potential issue: If L=0, dfs(s,0,start_bit) records. Good.

Now, combine:
pref = enumerate_walks(a, False)
suff = enumerate_walks(b, True)
ans = 0
for mid in empty_indices:
    dp = pref[mid]
    if not dp: continue
    ds = suff[mid]
    if not ds: continue
    # iterate smaller outer
    if len(dp) <= len(ds):
        for mp, cp in dp.items():
            for mq, cq in ds.items():
                if (mp & mq) == 0:
                    ans += cp * cq
    else:
        for mq, cq in ds.items():
            for mp, cp in dp.items():
                if (mp & mq) == 0:
                    ans += cp * cq
print(ans)

Let's write final code carefully:

import sys

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.readline
    H, W, K = map(int, input().split())
    S = [input().strip() for _ in range(H)]
    N = H * W
    dirs = [(1,0), (-1,0), (0,1), (0,-1)]
    empty = []
    neighbors = [[] for _ in range(N)]
    for i in range(H):
        for j in range(W):
            if S[i][j] == '.':
                idx = i * W + j
                empty.append(idx)
                for di, dj in dirs:
                    ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                    if 0 <= ni < H and 0 <= nj < W and S[ni][nj] == '.':
                        neighbors[idx].append(ni * W + nj)
    bit = [1 << i for i in range(N)]

    sys.setrecursionlimit(10000)

    def enumerate_walks(L, remove_start):
        groups = [dict() for _ in range(N)]
        neigh = neighbors
        bits = bit
        for s in empty:
            start_bit = bits[s]
            def dfs(v, depth, mask):
                if depth == L:
                    if remove_start:
                        key = mask ^ start_bit
                        d = groups[s]
                    else:
                        key = mask ^ bits[v]
                        d = groups[v]
                    d[key] = d.get(key, 0) + 1
                    return
                for to in neigh[v]:
                    if not (mask & bits[to]):
                        dfs(to, depth + 1, mask | bits[to])
            dfs(s, 0, start_bit)
        return groups

    a = K // 2
    b = K - a
    pref = enumerate_walks(a, False)
    suff = enumerate_walks(b, True)

    ans = 0
    for mid in empty:
        dp = pref[mid]
        if not dp:
            continue
        ds = suff[mid]
        if not ds:
            continue
        if len(dp) <= len(ds):
            for mp, cp in dp.items():
                for mq, cq in ds.items():
                    if (mp & mq) == 0:
                        ans += cp * cq
        else:
            for mq, cq in ds.items():
                for mp, cp in dp.items():
                    if (mp & mq) == 0:
                        ans += cp * cq
    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

But there is a subtle issue with Python's recursion and closures: The inner `dfs` references `groups`, `neigh`, `bits`, `L`, `remove_start`, `s`, `start_bit`. Since `s` changes in the for loop, but `dfs` is called before `s` changes. However, Python's closure captures the variable `s` by reference, not value. The function `dfs` is defined in the loop body; it is called immediately within the same iteration, so `s` has the current value. After the call returns, the function object may still exist? Actually no reference to it after the call? The recursive calls refer to the same function object; after the top-level call returns, the function object is no longer referenced (unless recursion stack? stack unwound). So no issue. But to be safe and clearer, we can define `dfs` as a nested function inside `enumerate_walks` with parameters `s, start_bit`? Let's consider.

If we define:

def enumerate_walks(L, remove_start):
    groups = [dict() for _ in range(N)]
    def dfs(s, v, depth, mask):
        if depth == L:
            if remove_start:
                key = mask ^ bit[s]
                d = groups[s]
            else:
                key = mask ^ bit[v]
                d = groups[v]
            d[key] = d.get(key, 0) + 1
            return
        for to in neighbors[v]:
            if not (mask & bit[to]):
                dfs(s, to, depth+1, mask | bit[to])
    for s in empty:
        dfs(s, s, 0, bit[s])
    return groups

This avoids defining dfs per start and captures s as parameter. It passes s through recursion. This is cleaner and maybe faster? It adds an extra parameter to each recursive call. This works.

Let's rewrite:

def enumerate_walks(L, remove_start):
    groups = [dict() for _ in range(N)]
    neigh = neighbors
    bits = bit
    def dfs(start, v, depth, mask):
        if depth == L:
            if remove_start:
                key = mask ^ bits[start]
                d = groups[start]
            else:
                key = mask ^ bits[v]
                d = groups[v]
            d[key] = d.get(key, 0) + 1
            return
        for to in neigh[v]:
            if not (mask & bits[to]):
                dfs(start, to, depth + 1, mask | bits[to])
    for s in empty:
        dfs(s, s, 0, bits[s])
    return groups

This is better. For prefix, `start` is full start, `v` is mid. For suffix, `start` is mid, `v` is end. Good.

Let's ensure MITM code doesn't have a bug in the `enumerate_walks` function due to using `remove_start` boolean. Let's formalize:

For a walk of length L starting at `start`, ending at `v`, with visited mask `mask`:
- If `remove_start` is False (prefix): We want to group by the ending cell `v` (which will be the middle of the full path). The key should be all visited cells except `v`, because the suffix will also contain `v` and we don't want to count it as overlap. So key = mask without bit v. Good.
- If `remove_start` is True (suffix): We want to group by the starting cell `start` (which is the middle). The key should be all visited cells except `start`. So key = mask without bit start. Good.

In combine, for a fixed middle cell `m`, we take prefix walks ending at `m` and suffix walks starting at `m`. The prefix key excludes m, suffix key excludes m. If keys are disjoint, the only common cell is m. Good.

Now, what about the prefix's start cell and suffix's end cell? They are included in keys. If they are the same, keys share bit, condition fails. Good.

Full path starts at m, then suffix moves; m appears only at start. Valid. If a>0, prefix start cannot be m due to self-avoiding. Good.

Could also precompute list of items to avoid repeated `.items()`? In Python, `for mp, cp in dp.items():` creates a view; inner `for mq, cq in ds.items():` creates a view each outer iteration? Actually `ds.items()` is called each time the inner loop starts? In the code:
for mp, cp in dp.items():
    for mq, cq in ds.items():
        ...
The inner `ds.items()` is evaluated once per outer iteration? In Python, the iterable expression is evaluated once at the start of the for loop, so for each outer iteration, it creates a new view? Let's recall: `for mq, cq in ds.items():` evaluates `ds.items()` each time the outer loop body is entered? Actually the for statement evaluates the expression once when the loop begins. Since the inner for is inside the outer loop body, it is executed multiple times; each execution evaluates `ds.items()` anew. This creates a dict_items view, which is cheap but not free. To optimize, we can convert to list of items: `ds_items = list(ds.items())` before outer loop, and `dp_items = list(dp.items())`. Then iterate over lists. This avoids creating views repeatedly. Similarly if swapping. Let's implement this optimization.

Combine:
dp_items = list(dp.items())
ds_items = list(ds.items())
if len(dp_items) <= len(ds_items):
    for mp, cp in dp_items:
        for mq, cq in ds_items:
            if (mp & mq) == 0:
                ans += cp * cq
else:
    for mq, cq in ds_items:
        for mp, cp in dp_items:
            if (mp & mq) == 0:
                ans += cp * cq

This is slightly faster. We'll use this.

=== ANSWER ===
? I need output final code. The above reasoning is too long but final code is what matters. Let's produce final code block with MITM solution. Ensure no extraneous text.</think>```python
import sys

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.readline
    H, W, K = map(int, input().split())
    S = [input().strip() for _ in range(H)]

    N = H * W
    dirs = [(1, 0), (-1, 0), (0, 1), (0, -1)]

    empty = []
    neighbors = [[] for _ in range(N)]

    for i in range(H):
        for j in range(W):
            if S[i][j] == '.':
                idx = i * W + j
                empty.append(idx)
                for di, dj in dirs:
                    ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                    if 0 <= ni < H and 0 <= nj < W and S[ni][nj] == '.':
                        neighbors[idx].append(ni * W + nj)

    bit = [1 << i for i in range(N)]
    sys.setrecursionlimit(10000)

    def enumerate_walks(L, remove_start):
        groups = [dict() for _ in range(N)]
        neigh = neighbors
        bits = bit

        def dfs(start, v, depth, mask):
            if depth == L:
                if remove_start:
                    key = mask ^ bits[start]
                    d = groups[start]
                else:
                    key = mask ^ bits[v]
                    d = groups[v]
                d[key] = d.get(key, 0) + 1
                return

            for to in neigh[v]:
                if not (mask & bits[to]):
                    dfs(start, to, depth + 1, mask | bits[to])

        for s in empty:
            dfs(s, s, 0, bits[s])

        return groups

    a = K // 2
    b = K - a

    pref = enumerate_walks(a, False)
    suff = enumerate_walks(b, True)

    ans = 0

    for mid in empty:
        dp = pref[mid]
        if not dp:
            continue
        ds = suff[mid]
        if not ds:
            continue

        dp_items = list(dp.items())
        ds_items = list(ds.items())

        if len(dp_items) <= len(ds_items):
            for mp, cp in dp_items:
                for mq, cq in ds_items:
                    if (mp & mq) == 0:
                        ans += cp * cq
        else:
            for mq, cq in ds_items:
                for mp, cp in dp_items:
                    if (mp & mq) == 0:
                        ans += cp * cq

    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
